<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove Serre's positivity conjecture, including ramified mixed characteristic. If two nonzero finitely generated modules over a regular local ring have tensor product of finite length and complementary dimensions, then their intersection multiplicity is strictly positive.

- **claim-serre-positivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $(R,\mathfrak{m})$ be a commutative Noetherian regular local ring of dimension $d$, and let $M,N$ be nonzero finitely generated $R$-modules. If $\operatorname{length}_R(M\otimes_R N)<\infty$ and $\dim_R M+\dim_R N=d$, then $$\chi^R(M,N)>0.$$
  - Explanation: For nonzero finitely generated modules over a regular local ring with finite-length tensor product and complementary dimensions, Serre's intersection multiplicity is strictly positive. No restriction on characteristic or ramification is imposed.
- **claim-finite-strict-transform-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-tangent-cone)): Let $(A,\mathfrak{m})$ be a commutative Noetherian regular local ring with residue field $k$, and let $P,Q$ be prime ideals such that $A/(P+Q)$ has finite length and $\dim(A/P)+\dim(A/Q)=\dim A$. Write $e_\mathfrak{m}$ for Hilbert–Samuel multiplicity and put $$T=\operatorname{gr}(A/P)\otimes_{\operatorname{gr}A}\operatorname{gr}(A/Q),$$ where all associated graded rings use the maximal-ideal filtrations. If $\dim T\leq1$, then $$\chi^A(A/P,A/Q)\geq e_\mathfrak{m}(A/P)e_\mathfrak{m}(A/Q),$$ with equality if and only if $\dim T=0$.
  - Explanation: For prime quotients with finite-length sum and complementary dimension, if the tensor product of the associated graded rings has dimension at most one, the intersection multiplicity is bounded below by the product of Hilbert–Samuel multiplicities. Equality holds exactly when that tensor product has dimension zero.
- **claim-residue-field-extension** (construction, [source](#stmt-len-residue-extension)): Let $(B,\mathfrak b,k)$ be a complete Noetherian local ring, and let $\overline{k}$ be an algebraic closure of $k$. There is a complete Noetherian local ring $(B',\mathfrak b',\overline{k})$ and a flat local map $B\to B'$ such that $\mathfrak bB'=\mathfrak b'$. For every finite $B$-module $V$ and every $a\geq1$, $$\operatorname{length}_{B'}\bigl((V\otimes_B B')/(\mathfrak b')^a (V\otimes_B B')\bigr) =\operatorname{length}_B(V/\mathfrak b^aV).$$ Consequently module dimensions are preserved, as are finite lengths. If $B$ is regular, then $B'$ is regular. For finite $B$-modules $M,N$ whose tensor product has finite length, every Tor length, and hence their intersection multiplicity when defined, is preserved.
  - Explanation: A complete Noetherian local ring admits a flat local extension with algebraically closed residue field that preserves lengths of quotients by powers of the maximal ideal, module dimensions, regularity, and Tor lengths.
- **claim-regular-normalization-base** (construction, [source](#stmt-len-normalization)): Let $(D,\mathfrak d,k)$ be a complete Noetherian local domain of dimension $h>0$, where $k$ is perfect of characteristic $p>0$. There is a finite, generically separable inclusion $A\subseteq D$, inducing the identity on residue fields, with $$A=k[[t_1,\ldots,t_h]]\quad\text{if }\operatorname{char}D=p, \qquad A=W(k)[[t_2,\ldots,t_h]]\quad\text{if }\operatorname{char}D=0.$$ In the second case set $t_1=p$. In both cases $t_1,\ldots,t_h$ is a parameter system of $D$ and a regular parameter system of $A$.
  - Explanation: A complete local domain with perfect residue field is finite and generically separable over a power-series ring, or a Witt-vector power-series ring in characteristic zero. The chosen variables form a parameter system of the domain and a regular parameter system of the base.
- **claim-tower-length** (construction, [source](#stmt-len-tower)): Each $A_n$ is a complete regular local ring with residue field $k$. For $b\geq a$, the map $A_a\to A_b$ is finite free of rank $p^{(b-a)h}$; in particular $A\to A_n$ has rank $p^{nh}$. On the category of $\Lambda$-modules killed by some power of $\mathfrak m_A$ there is a nonnegative additive function $\lambda_\infty$ with the following properties.* 1. *A finitely presented module descends to a finite-length $A_n$-module $V_n$, and $$\label{len:finite-stage} \lambda_\infty(V_n\otimes_{A_n}\Lambda) =p^{-nh}\operatorname{length}_{A_n}(V_n).$$* 2. *For a finitely generated module $V$, its length is the infimum of the lengths of finitely presented modules in this category admitting a surjection onto $V$.* 3. *For an arbitrary module $V$ in this category, $$\label{len:supremum} \lambda_\infty(V)= \sup\{\lambda_\infty(V_0):V_0\subseteq V \text{ is finitely generated}\}.$$* *The value on an arbitrary module may be infinite. In mixed characteristic, restriction from $A_\infty$ to $\Lambda$ gives the same length on modules killed by a fixed power of $\mathfrak m_A$.
  - Explanation: The tower of parameter roots consists of complete regular local rings that are finite free over each other. It carries a nonnegative additive normalized length determined on finite stages and extended by infimum and supremum.
- **claim-zero-length-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-len-zero-length-limits)): Suppose all modules under consideration are killed by one fixed power of $\mathfrak m_A$. Sums, direct sums, and directed colimits of zero-length modules have length zero. For an increasing union, $$\lambda_\infty\Bigl(\bigcup_a V_a\Bigr) =\sup_a\lambda_\infty(V_a).$$ If $(V_a)_{a\geq1}$ is a direct system with $\lambda_\infty(V_a)\to0$, then $\lambda_\infty(\varinjlim_a V_a)=0$, without any injectivity assumption on the transition maps.
  - Explanation: Under a common annihilating power, sums, direct sums and directed colimits of zero-length modules have zero length, and length of an increasing union is the supremum of the lengths of the stages.
- **claim-normalized-length-input** (construction, [source](#stmt-len-input)): Let $(D,\mathfrak d,k)$ and a chosen finite generically separable base $A\subseteq D$ be as in Lemma <a href="#len:normalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="len:normalization">4</a>, and let $\Lambda$ be the tower <a href="#len:tower-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="len:tower-definition">[len:tower-definition]</a>. There is a $D$-algebra $C_D$, carrying a compatible $\Lambda$-action, such that restriction of scalars defines an additive nonnegative length $\lambda_D=\lambda_\infty$ on its modules annihilated by a power of $\mathfrak d$. For every parameter system $z_1,\ldots,z_h$ of $D$ and every integer $v\geq1$, $$\begin{aligned} \lambda_D H_i(z_1^v,\ldots,z_h^v;C_D)&=0\qquad(i>0), \label{len:koszul-vanishing}\\ \lambda_D\bigl(C_D/(z_1^v,\ldots,z_h^v)C_D\bigr) &=v^h e((z_1,\ldots,z_h),D). \label{len:parameter-length} \end{aligned}$$ The right side is positive and finite. The algebra and length are independent of the parameter system $z$. For the regular parameters of $A$, in particular, $$\label{len:base-parameter-length} \lambda_D\bigl(C_D/(t_1^v,\ldots,t_h^v)C_D\bigr) =v^h\operatorname{rank}_A D.$$ The finite-stage, infimum, and supremum properties of Lemma <a href="#len:tower" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="len:tower">5</a> remain available on underlying tower modules; they do not require that the relevant submodules or covers be $C_D$-modules.
  - Explanation: There is an algebra over the domain with a compatible tower action and a nonnegative additive length on which higher parameter Koszul homology has length zero and parameter quotients have length given by Hilbert–Samuel multiplicity, which equals the extension rank for the base parameters.
- **claim-finite-length-base-change** (theorem, [source](#stmt-len-finite-length-base-change)): In Theorem <a href="#len:input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="len:input">7</a>, put $r=\operatorname{rank}_A D$. For every finite-length $A$-module $V$, $$\lambda_D\operatorname{Tor}_i^A(V,C_D)=0\quad(i>0),\qquad \lambda_D(V\otimes_A C_D)=r\operatorname{length}_A(V).$$ Moreover $\lambda_D(C_D/\mathfrak d C_D)>0$.
  - Explanation: Against the input algebra, positive Tor groups of finite-length base modules have zero normalized length, degree-zero length is the rank times the base length, and the residue quotient has positive normalized length.
- **claim-supported-rational-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ds-descent)): Let $A$ be a commutative Noetherian $\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}$-algebra of finite Krull dimension, let $J\subset A$ be an ideal containing $p$, and let $H$ be a connective associative $A$-algebra with central $A$-action. Let $T$ be a finite commutative $A$-algebra with an action of a finite group $G$ over $A$. Suppose that $\operatorname{Spec}T\to\operatorname{Spec}A$ is surjective and that $G$ acts transitively on the points of every geometric fiber. Then pullback induces an equivalence $$K^J(H)_\mathbb{Q} \simeq \bigl(K^J(H\otimes_A^{\mathbf L}T)_\mathbb{Q}\bigr)^{hG}. \label{ds:spectral-descent}$$ In particular, the map $$K_0^J(H)\otimes\mathbb{Q} \longrightarrow \bigl(K_0^J(H\otimes_A^{\mathbf L}T)\otimes\mathbb{Q}\bigr)^G \label{ds:kzero-descent}$$ is surjective.
  - Explanation: For a finite surjective algebra with a finite group acting transitively on geometric fibers, supported rational K-theory of connective associative coefficients is the homotopy fixed points of the K-theory over the cover. The degree-zero map to invariants is surjective.
- **claim-descent-finite-flat** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ds-finite-flat)): The functor $\mathcal{L}$ satisfies ordinary Čech descent for finite faithfully flat covers, universally under base change.
  - Explanation: The supported rational K-theory functor satisfies Čech descent along finite faithfully flat covers, via a rational transfer that makes it a retract of the cover's value.
- **claim-descent-finite-surjection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ds-finite-surjection)): The functor $\mathcal{L}$ satisfies ordinary Čech descent for every finite surjection of finite-type $A$-schemes.
  - Explanation: Descent extends to every finite surjection by combining cdh descent, flattening by blowup and induction on closed subsets.
- **claim-formal-finite-length-base-change** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ds-finite-length-basechange)): For every finite-length $A$-module $V$, $$\lambda\operatorname{Tor}_i^A(V,C)=0\quad(i>0), \qquad \lambda(V\otimes_A C)=r\operatorname{length}_A(V). \label{ds:finite-length-formula}$$ All these lengths are finite.
  - Explanation: Using only the length hypotheses, positive Tor groups against the length algebra have zero length and degree-zero length is the rank times the base length, with all lengths finite.
- **claim-ordinary-norm-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ds-ordinary-norm)): Assume, in addition, that a finite group $G$ acts on $T$ over $A$ and is transitive on every geometric fiber of $\operatorname{Spec}T\to\operatorname{Spec}A$. For a perfect $T$-complex $P$ supported on $V(\mathfrak{m}_A T)$, the quantities $$\chi_T(P)=\sum_i(-1)^i\operatorname{length}_T H_i(P), \qquad \chi_C(P)=\sum_i(-1)^i \lambda H_i(P\otimes_T^{\mathbf L}C)$$ are finite and define homomorphisms on $K_0^{\mathfrak{m}_A}(T)$. They agree on every norm sum: $$\sum_{g\in G}\chi_T(g^*P) =\sum_{g\in G}\chi_C(g^*P). \label{ds:ordinary-norm-identity}$$ More generally, the two homomorphisms agree on the image of $K_0^{\mathfrak{m}_A}(A)$ and on every $G$-invariant rational class.
  - Explanation: For perfect complexes supported at the closed point, the ordinary and normalized Euler evaluations are finite and agree after summing over all conjugates by the group, and more generally on invariant rational classes.
- **claim-supported-subquotient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-as-supported-quotient)): The natural exact functor $$\mathcal S_S/(\mathcal S_S\cap\mathcal N_S) \longrightarrow \mathcal D_S/\mathcal N_S$$ is fully faithful. Its idempotent completion is $\mathcal P_S^J$.
  - Explanation: The supported subquotient embeds fully faithfully into the rank quotient, and its idempotent completion is the category of supported objects.
- **claim-supported-evaluations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-as-evaluations)): There are homological functors $$\mathsf H_i^{\mathrm{ord}}:\mathcal P_T^J \longrightarrow\overline{\mathcal A}_T, \qquad \mathsf H_i^C:\mathcal P_T^J \longrightarrow\overline{\mathcal A}_C$$ whose values on a uniformly supported lift are its ordinary homology sequence and its homology sequence after tensoring with $C$, respectively. For each object $X$, their lengths $$b_i^{\mathrm{ord}}(X)=\overline\operatorname{length}_T \mathsf H_i^{\mathrm{ord}}(X), \qquad b_i^C(X)=\overline\lambda\mathsf H_i^C(X)$$ are finite nonnegative real numbers, zero outside a finite range. Their Euler sums define homomorphisms $$\chi_{\mathrm{ord}},\chi_C:K_0(\mathcal P_T^J)\longrightarrow\mathbb R.$$ If $F=(F_n)$ is a bounded free sequence whose image is supported, and $v$ makes all $z_j^v$ act as zero on that image, then for either choice of evaluation, writing $b_i(F)=b_i(q_TF)$, $$(1+t)^e\sum_i b_i(F)t^i =\sum_i t^i\lim_{n\to\mathcal U} \frac{\operatorname{len} H_i(K_v\otimes_A F_n)}{s_n}. \label{as:koszul-polynomial}$$ Here $\operatorname{len}=\operatorname{length}_T$ in the ordinary case; in the $C$ case the complex on the right is further tensored over $T$ with $C$, and $\operatorname{len}=\lambda$. The identity is one of finite Laurent polynomials.
  - Explanation: Supported objects of the rank quotient carry homological functors into length quotients, with finite nonnegative lengths, Euler homomorphisms, and a Koszul polynomial identity computed from uniformly supported lifts.
- **claim-asymptotic-finite-tor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-as-finite-tor)): For every finite-length $A$-module $V$, $$\lambda\operatorname{Tor}_j^A(V,C)=0\quad(j>0), \qquad \lambda(V\otimes_A C)=r\operatorname{length}_A V.$$ All these modules are annihilated by a power of $J$.
  - Explanation: For a not necessarily complete base, finite-length modules have zero normalized positive Tor length against the length algebra and degree-zero length equal to the rank times the base length.
- **claim-base-equality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-as-base-equality)): For every $X\in\mathcal P_A^J$, $$\chi_{\mathrm{ord}}(X\otimes_A^{\mathbf L}T) =r\chi_A(X) =\chi_C(X\otimes_A^{\mathbf L}T).$$ These equalities hold for summands defined by idempotents in the Verdier quotient, without assuming that the idempotents lift to chain maps before localization.
  - Explanation: On classes extended from the base, including idempotent summands that need not lift, the ordinary and normalized evaluations both equal the rank times the base evaluation.
- **claim-connective-coefficients** (theorem, [source](#stmt-as-connective)): There is a connective associative derived $A$-algebra $H$ and compatible equivalences $$\mathcal P_A\simeq\operatorname{Perf}(H), \qquad \mathcal P_T\simeq\operatorname{Perf}(H\otimes_A^{\mathbf L}T).$$ The equivalences respect central localization by elements of $A$.
  - Explanation: The rank-quotient categories are perfect module categories over a connective derived algebra and its base change, compatibly with central localization.
- **claim-asymptotic-norm-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-as-norm)): Let $A,J,T,C,\lambda$ satisfy the setup of this section and <a href="#as:length-hypotheses" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="as:length-hypotheses">[as:length-hypotheses]</a>. Suppose a finite group $G$ acts on $T$ over $A$, the map $\operatorname{Spec}T\to\operatorname{Spec}A$ is surjective, and $G$ is transitive on every geometric fiber. Then, for every $X\in\mathcal P_T^J$, $$\sum_{g\in G}\chi_{\mathrm{ord}}(gX) =\sum_{g\in G}\chi_C(gX).$$ Neither invariance of $\lambda$ under $G$ nor equality of the two evaluations on individual classes is assumed.
  - Explanation: For every supported object, the sums over group conjugates of the ordinary and normalized Euler evaluations agree. No invariance of the length and no equality on individual classes is assumed.
- **claim-reduction-to-domains** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pos-reduction)): To prove Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, it suffices to prove the assertion for $$D=R/P,\qquad E=R/Q,\qquad h=\dim D>0,\qquad l=\dim E>0,\qquad h+l=d,$$ where $R$ is complete with algebraically closed residue field $k$ of characteristic $p>0$, $P,Q$ are prime, and $P+Q$ is primary to $\mathfrak{m}_R$.
  - Explanation: It suffices to treat quotients by primes of positive complementary dimensions over a complete ring with algebraically closed residue field of positive characteristic, with sum primary to the maximal ideal.
- **claim-first-galois-average** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pos-first-average)): The lengths in Equation <a href="#pos:chi-sigma" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pos:chi-sigma">[pos:chi-sigma]</a> are finite, and $$\label{pos:first-average-equation} \|G_D\|a_Da_E\,\chi^R(D,E)=\sum_{\sigma\in G_D}c_\sigma.$$
  - Explanation: The normalized Tor Euler characteristics are finite, and their sum over the first Galois group equals the intersection multiplicity scaled by the group order and the two ranks.
- **claim-tor-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pos-tor-bound)): For every finite $B$-module $L$, all the following lengths are finite, and $$\label{pos:tor-identification} \tau_i(L):=\lambda\bigl(\operatorname{Tor}_i^B(U,L)\bigr) =\lambda\bigl(\operatorname{Tor}_i^R(C,L)\bigr), \qquad \tau_i(L)=0\quad(i>l).$$
  - Explanation: For finite modules over the parameter quotient, normalized Tor lengths over it agree with those over the ring and vanish above the codimension degree.
- **claim-finite-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-pos-approximation)): There are finite-length $B$-modules $M_n$ and positive integers $s_n\to\infty$, with $\mathfrak{m}_B^aM_n=0$, such that $$\begin{aligned} \frac{\operatorname{length}_B(M_n)}{s_n}&\longrightarrow\lambda(U), \label{pos:approximation-length}\\ \frac{\operatorname{length}_B\operatorname{Tor}_i^B(M_n,L)}{s_n} &\longrightarrow\tau_i(L) \quad(L=k,E',\ i\ge0). \label{pos:approximation-tor} \end{aligned}$$ Writing $\beta_0(M_n)=\dim_k(M_n/\mathfrak{m}_BM_n)$, we also have $$\label{pos:generator-bound} \frac{\beta_0(M_n)}{s_n}\longrightarrow\nu_\sigma, \qquad \nu_\sigma\ge \frac{\lambda(U)}{\operatorname{length}_B(B/\mathfrak{m}_B^a)}>0.$$
  - Explanation: Finite-length modules killed by one power of the maximal ideal approximate the normalized lengths and Tor lengths, with a positive lower bound on the normalized number of generators.
- **claim-supported-truncation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pos-supported-truncation)): The sequence $(F_{n,\gamma}')_n$ is supported at the closed point in that category. Its ordinary Euler evaluation is $c_\sigma$ for every $\gamma\in G_E$.
  - Explanation: The truncated extended resolutions are supported at the closed point in the rank quotient, and their ordinary Euler evaluation equals the first-average term for every conjugate.
- **claim-positive-normalized-evaluation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pos-positive-evaluation)): The $\lambda_E$ Euler evaluation of $(F_{n,\gamma}')_n$ is a number $b_{\sigma,\gamma}$ satisfying $$\label{pos:strict-bound} b_{\sigma,\gamma} \ge\nu_\sigma\eta_E \ge \frac{\lambda_D(U)}{\operatorname{length}_B(B/\mathfrak{m}_B^a)} \frac{r_E}{t_E} >0.$$
  - Explanation: The normalized Euler evaluation of the truncated sequence collapses to degree zero and is bounded below by a strictly positive quantity built from the generator bound and residue length.

For an increasing union, $$\\lambda_\\infty\\Bigl(\\bigcup_a V_a\\Bigr)\n                         =\\sup_a\\lambda_\\infty(V_a).$$ If $(V_a)_{a\\geq1}$ is a direct system with $\\lambda_\\infty(V_a)\\to0$, then $\\lambda_\\infty(\\varinjlim_a V_a)=0$, without any injectivity assumption on the transition maps.",
      "explanation": "Under a common annihilating power, sums, direct sums and directed colimits of zero-length modules have zero length, and length of an increasing union is the supremum of the lengths of the stages.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-len-zero-length-limits",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose all modules under consideration are killed by one fixed power of $\\mathfrak m_A$.",
          "source_id": "PM-193.1"
        }

      "id": "claim-zero-length-limits",

      "quantifiers": [],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-len-input",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(D,\\mathfrak d,k)$ and a chosen finite generically separable base $A\\subseteq D$ be as in Lemma <a href=\"#len:normalization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"len:normalization\">4</a>, and let $\\Lambda$ be the tower <a href=\"#len:tower-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"len:tower-definition\">[len:tower-definition]</a>. There is a $D$-algebra $C_D$, carrying a compatible $\\Lambda$-action, such that restriction of scalars defines an additive nonnegative length $\\lambda_D=\\lambda_\\infty$ on its modules annihilated by a power of $\\mathfrak d$. For every parameter system $z_1,\\ldots,z_h$ of $D$ and every integer $v\\geq1$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\lambda_D H_i(z_1^v,\\ldots,z_h^v;C_D)&=0\\qquad(i>0),\n                                      \\label{len:koszul-vanishing}\\\\\n \\lambda_D\\bigl(C_D/(z_1^v,\\ldots,z_h^v)C_D\\bigr)\n                    &=v^h e((z_1,\\ldots,z_h),D).\n                                      \\label{len:parameter-length}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The right side is positive and finite. The algebra and length are independent of the parameter system $z$. For the regular parameters of $A$, in particular, $$\\label{len:base-parameter-length}\n \\lambda_D\\bigl(C_D/(t_1^v,\\ldots,t_h^v)C_D\\bigr)\n                         =v^h\\operatorname{rank}_A D.$$ The finite-stage, infimum, and supremum properties of Lemma <a href=\"#len:tower\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"len:tower\">5</a> remain available on underlying tower modules; they do not require that the relevant submodules or covers be $C_D$-modules.",
        "source_id": "PM-193.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-len-input",

There is a $D$-algebra $C_D$, carrying a compatible $\\Lambda$-action, such that restriction of scalars defines an additive nonnegative length $\\lambda_D=\\lambda_\\infty$ on its modules annihilated by a power of $\\mathfrak d$. For every parameter system $z_1,\\ldots,z_h$ of $D$ and every integer $v\\geq1$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\lambda_D H_i(z_1^v,\\ldots,z_h^v;C_D)&=0\\qquad(i>0),\n                                      \\label{len:koszul-vanishing}\\\\\n \\lambda_D\\bigl(C_D/(z_1^v,\\ldots,z_h^v)C_D\\bigr)\n                    &=v^h e((z_1,\\ldots,z_h),D).\n                                      \\label{len:parameter-length}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The right side is positive and finite. The algebra and length are independent of the parameter system $z$. For the regular parameters of $A$, in particular, $$\\label{len:base-parameter-length}\n \\lambda_D\\bigl(C_D/(t_1^v,\\ldots,t_h^v)C_D\\bigr)\n                         =v^h\\operatorname{rank}_A D.$$ The finite-stage, infimum, and supremum properties of Lemma <a href=\"#len:tower\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"len:tower\">5</a> remain available on underlying tower modules; they do not require that the relevant submodules or covers be $C_D$-modules.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ds-descent",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a commutative Noetherian $\\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}$-algebra of finite Krull dimension, let $J\\subset A$ be an ideal containing $p$, and let $H$ be a connective associative $A$-algebra with central $A$-action. Let $T$ be a finite commutative $A$-algebra with an action of a finite group $G$ over $A$. Suppose that $\\operatorname{Spec}T\\to\\operatorname{Spec}A$ is surjective and that $G$ acts transitively on the points of every geometric fiber. Then pullback induces an equivalence $$K^J(H)_\\mathbb{Q}\n \\simeq\n \\bigl(K^J(H\\otimes_A^{\\mathbf L}T)_\\mathbb{Q}\\bigr)^{hG}.\n \\label{ds:spectral-descent}$$ In particular, the map $$K_0^J(H)\\otimes\\mathbb{Q}\n \\longrightarrow\n \\bigl(K_0^J(H\\otimes_A^{\\mathbf L}T)\\otimes\\mathbb{Q}\\bigr)^G\n \\label{ds:kzero-descent}$$ is surjective.",
        "source_id": "PM-193.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ds-descent",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a commutative Noetherian $\\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}$-algebra of finite Krull dimension, let $J\\subset A$ be an ideal containing $p$, and let $H$ be a connective associative $A$-algebra with central $A$-action. Let $T$ be a finite commutative $A$-algebra with an action of a finite group $G$ over $A$. Suppose that $\\operatorname{Spec}T\\to\\operatorname{Spec}A$ is surjective and that $G$ acts transitively on the points of every geometric fiber. Then pullback induces an equivalence $$K^J(H)_\\mathbb{Q}\n \\simeq\n \\bigl(K^J(H\\otimes_A^{\\mathbf L}T)_\\mathbb{Q}\\bigr)^{hG}.\n \\label{ds:spectral-descent}$$ In particular, the map $$K_0^J(H)\\otimes\\mathbb{Q}\n \\longrightarrow\n \\bigl(K_0^J(H\\otimes_A^{\\mathbf L}T)\\otimes\\mathbb{Q}\\bigr)^G\n \\label{ds:kzero-descent}$$ is surjective.",
          "source_id": "PM-193.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ds-ordinary-norm",
        "exact_quote": "Assume, in addition, that a finite group $G$ acts on $T$ over $A$ and is transitive on every geometric fiber of $\\operatorname{Spec}T\\to\\operatorname{Spec}A$. For a perfect $T$-complex $P$ supported on $V(\\mathfrak{m}_A T)$, the quantities $$\\chi_T(P)=\\sum_i(-1)^i\\operatorname{length}_T H_i(P),\n \\qquad\n \\chi_C(P)=\\sum_i(-1)^i\n           \\lambda H_i(P\\otimes_T^{\\mathbf L}C)$$ are finite and define homomorphisms on $K_0^{\\mathfrak{m}_A}(T)$. They agree on every norm sum: $$\\sum_{g\\in G}\\chi_T(g^*P)\n   =\\sum_{g\\in G}\\chi_C(g^*P).\n \\label{ds:ordinary-norm-identity}$$ More generally, the two homomorphisms agree on the image of $K_0^{\\mathfrak{m}_A}(A)$ and on every $G$-invariant rational class.",
        "source_id": "PM-193.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ds-ordinary-norm",
          "exact_quote": "Assume, in addition, that a finite group $G$ acts on $T$ over $A$ and is transitive on every geometric fiber of $\\operatorname{Spec}T\\to\\operatorname{Spec}A$. For a perfect $T$-complex $P$ supported on $V(\\mathfrak{m}_A T)$, the quantities $$\\chi_T(P)=\\sum_i(-1)^i\\operatorname{length}_T H_i(P),\n \\qquad\n \\chi_C(P)=\\sum_i(-1)^i\n           \\lambda H_i(P\\otimes_T^{\\mathbf L}C)$$ are finite and define homomorphisms on $K_0^{\\mathfrak{m}_A}(T)$. They agree on every norm sum: $$\\sum_{g\\in G}\\chi_T(g^*P)\n   =\\sum_{g\\in G}\\chi_C(g^*P).\n \\label{ds:ordinary-norm-identity}$$ More generally, the two homomorphisms agree on the image of $K_0^{\\mathfrak{m}_A}(A)$ and on every $G$-invariant rational class.",
          "source_id": "PM-193.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Assume, in addition, that a finite group $G$ acts on $T$ over $A$ and is transitive on every geometric fiber of $\\operatorname{Spec}T\\to\\operatorname{Spec}A$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-as-evaluations",
        "exact_quote": "There are homological functors $$\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathrm{ord}}:\\mathcal P_T^J\n       \\longrightarrow\\overline{\\mathcal A}_T,\n \\qquad\n \\mathsf H_i^C:\\mathcal P_T^J\n       \\longrightarrow\\overline{\\mathcal A}_C$$ whose values on a uniformly supported lift are its ordinary homology sequence and its homology sequence after tensoring with $C$, respectively. For each object $X$, their lengths $$b_i^{\\mathrm{ord}}(X)=\\overline\\operatorname{length}_T\n       \\mathsf H_i^{\\mathrm{ord}}(X),\n \\qquad\n b_i^C(X)=\\overline\\lambda\\mathsf H_i^C(X)$$ are finite nonnegative real numbers, zero outside a finite range. Their Euler sums define homomorphisms $$\\chi_{\\mathrm{ord}},\\chi_C:K_0(\\mathcal P_T^J)\\longrightarrow\\mathbb R.$$ If $F=(F_n)$ is a bounded free sequence whose image is supported, and $v$ makes all $z_j^v$ act as zero on that image, then for either choice of evaluation, writing $b_i(F)=b_i(q_TF)$, $$(1+t)^e\\sum_i b_i(F)t^i\n  =\\sum_i t^i\\lim_{n\\to\\mathcal U}\n     \\frac{\\operatorname{len}\n       H_i(K_v\\otimes_A F_n)}{s_n}.\n \\label{as:koszul-polynomial}$$ Here $\\operatorname{len}=\\operatorname{length}_T$ in the ordinary case; in the $C$ case the complex on the right is further tensored over $T$ with $C$, and $\\operatorname{len}=\\lambda$. The identity is one of finite Laurent polynomials.",
        "source_id": "PM-193.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-as-evaluations",
          "exact_quote": "There are homological functors $$\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathrm{ord}}:\\mathcal P_T^J\n       \\longrightarrow\\overline{\\mathcal A}_T,\n \\qquad\n \\mathsf H_i^C:\\mathcal P_T^J\n       \\longrightarrow\\overline{\\mathcal A}_C$$ whose values on a uniformly supported lift are its ordinary homology sequence and its homology sequence after tensoring with $C$, respectively. For each object $X$, their lengths $$b_i^{\\mathrm{ord}}(X)=\\overline\\operatorname{length}_T\n       \\mathsf H_i^{\\mathrm{ord}}(X),\n \\qquad\n b_i^C(X)=\\overline\\lambda\\mathsf H_i^C(X)$$ are finite nonnegative real numbers, zero outside a finite range. Their Euler sums define homomorphisms $$\\chi_{\\mathrm{ord}},\\chi_C:K_0(\\mathcal P_T^J)\\longrightarrow\\mathbb R.$$ If $F=(F_n)$ is a bounded free sequence whose image is supported, and $v$ makes all $z_j^v$ act as zero on that image, then for either choice of evaluation, writing $b_i(F)=b_i(q_TF)$, $$(1+t)^e\\sum_i b_i(F)t^i\n  =\\sum_i t^i\\lim_{n\\to\\mathcal U}\n     \\frac{\\operatorname{len}\n       H_i(K_v\\otimes_A F_n)}{s_n}.\n \\label{as:koszul-polynomial}$$ Here $\\operatorname{len}=\\operatorname{length}_T$ in the ordinary case; in the $C$ case the complex on the right is further tensored over $T$ with $C$, and $\\operatorname{len}=\\lambda$. The identity is one of finite Laurent polynomials.",
          "source_id": "PM-193.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are homological functors $$\\mathsf H_i^{\\mathrm{ord}}:\\mathcal P_T^J\n       \\longrightarrow\\overline{\\mathcal A}_T,\n \\qquad\n \\mathsf H_i^C:\\mathcal P_T^J\n       \\longrightarrow\\overline{\\mathcal A}_C$$ whose values on a uniformly supported lift are its ordinary homology sequence and its homology sequence after tensoring with $C$, respectively.       "conclusion": {

        "exact_quote": "Let $A,J,T,C,\\lambda$ satisfy the setup of this section and <a href=\"#as:length-hypotheses\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"as:length-hypotheses\">[as:length-hypotheses]</a>.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-pos-approximation",
        "exact_quote": "There are finite-length $B$-modules $M_n$ and positive integers $s_n\\to\\infty$, with $\\mathfrak{m}_B^aM_n=0$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\frac{\\operatorname{length}_B(M_n)}{s_n}&\\longrightarrow\\lambda(U),\n       \\label{pos:approximation-length}\\\\\n \\frac{\\operatorname{length}_B\\operatorname{Tor}_i^B(M_n,L)}{s_n}\n       &\\longrightarrow\\tau_i(L)\n       \\quad(L=k,E',\\ i\\ge0).\n       \\label{pos:approximation-tor}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Writing $\\beta_0(M_n)=\\dim_k(M_n/\\mathfrak{m}_BM_n)$, we also have $$\\label{pos:generator-bound}\n \\frac{\\beta_0(M_n)}{s_n}\\longrightarrow\\nu_\\sigma,\n \\qquad\n \\nu_\\sigma\\ge\n \\frac{\\lambda(U)}{\\operatorname{length}_B(B/\\mathfrak{m}_B^a)}>0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-193.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-193.1"

      "explanation": "The normalized Euler evaluation of the truncated sequence collapses to degree zero and is bounded below by a strictly positive quantity built from the generator bound and residue length.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-pos-positive-evaluation",
          "exact_quote": "The $\\lambda_E$ Euler evaluation of $(F_{n,\\gamma}')_n$ is a number $b_{\\sigma,\\gamma}$ satisfying $$\\label{pos:strict-bound}\n b_{\\sigma,\\gamma}\n    \\ge\\nu_\\sigma\\eta_E\n    \\ge\n       \\frac{\\lambda_D(U)}{\\operatorname{length}_B(B/\\mathfrak{m}_B^a)}\n       \\frac{r_E}{t_E}\n    >0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-193.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-positive-normalized-evaluation",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-pos-positive-evaluation"
    }
  ],

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **connective derived algebra** ([source](#pm-h-6)): An associative derived algebra is *connective* when its homotopy groups in negative degrees vanish.
- **Koszul complex notation** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For a sequence $a=(a_1,\ldots,a_r)$, write $K(a;V)$ for its homological Koszul complex with coefficients in $V$, and put $H_i(a;V)=H_i(K(a;V))$.
- **exponent notation for sequences** ([source](#pm-h-6)): The notation $a^v$ denotes $(a_1^v,\ldots,a_r^v)$, not the $v$th power of the ideal it generates.
- **Witt vector ring** ([source](#pm-h-9)): For a perfect field $k$ of characteristic $p$, let $W(k)$ denote its ring of Witt vectors.
- **Hilbert–Samuel multiplicity** ([source](#pm-h-10)): For an ideal $I$ primary to the maximal ideal of a local ring $D$, write $e(I,D)$ for its Hilbert–Samuel multiplicity.
- **truncating invariant** ([source](#pm-h-12)): It is called *truncating* if its value on a connective associative algebra is unchanged by passage to $\pi_0$.
- **localizing invariant** ([source](#pm-h-12)): A localizing invariant takes exact sequences of stable categories to fiber sequences.
- **cdh topology** ([source](#pm-h-12)): The cdh topology is generated by Nisnevich covers and abstract blowup squares: a proper map and a closed center, with the proper map an isomorphism off that center.
- **thick subcategory** ([source](#pm-h-17)): A thick subcategory is closed under shifts, cones, and direct summands.
- **rank quotient category** ([source](#pm-h-17)): Let $\mathcal N_S$ be the thick subcategory generated by the free sequences with $a_n=o(s_n)$, and define $$\mathcal P_S=(\mathcal D_S/\mathcal N_S)^{\natural}.$$
- **support on J** ([source](#pm-h-17)): An object $X$ in $\mathcal D_S$ or $\mathcal P_S$ is called *supported on $J$* if there is an integer $v\geq1$ such that $z_j^v\operatorname{id}_X=0$ for every $j$.
- **ramified** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Here ramified means that the residue characteristic $p$ lies in $\mathfrak{m}^2$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-residue-extension** [claim](#stmt-len-residue-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-len-residue-extension): Presentation by power series, flat residue-field extension in each characteristic, then length transfer along composition series. -> Flat local extension with algebraically closed residue field preserving lengths, dimensions, regularity and Tor lengths.
- **step-normalization** [claim](#stmt-len-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-len-normalization): Cohen–Gabber normalization in positive characteristic; coefficient ring and parameter extension in mixed characteristic. -> Finite generically separable regular base inside the domain.
- **step-tower** [claim](#stmt-len-tower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-len-tower): Power-series stages with monomial bases, descent of finite presentations, extension by infimum and supremum from normalized-length formalism. -> Additive normalized length on the tower.
- **step-zero-length-limits** [claim](#stmt-len-zero-length-limits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-len-zero-length-limits): Finite subsums and stages, additivity and the supremum formula. -> Zero-length stability under limits and unions.
- **step-length-input** [claim](#stmt-len-input) / [proof](#proof-stmt-len-input): Perfection with Hilbert–Kunz limits and Frobenius homology in characteristic p; perfectoidization with cited vanishing and multiplicity results in mixed characteristic. -> Input algebra with Koszul vanishing and multiplicity-valued parameter quotients.
- **step-length-base-change** [claim](#stmt-len-finite-length-base-change) / [proof](#proof-stmt-len-finite-length-base-change): Koszul resolution of the residue field, induction on composition series, and a filtration argument for positivity. -> Rank-scaled base change formula and positive residue length.
- **step-descent-finite-flat** [claim](#stmt-ds-finite-flat) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ds-finite-flat): Transfer and projection formula make the functor a rational retract; split descent from an extra degeneracy. -> Čech descent for finite faithfully flat covers.
- **step-descent-finite-surjection** [claim](#stmt-ds-finite-surjection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ds-finite-surjection): Combine cdh and finite flat covers, flatten by blowup, induct on dimension. -> Čech descent for finite surjections.
- **step-supported-descent** [claim](#stmt-ds-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ds-finite-surjection-2): Reduce to covers with a section, induct on dimension using translated sections and an abstract blowup square, take homotopy invariants. -> Equivalence with homotopy fixed points and surjectivity on invariants.
- **step-formal-base-change** [claim](#stmt-ds-finite-length-basechange) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ds-finite-length-basechange): Koszul resolution then induction over a composition series with long exact Tor sequences. -> Formal finite-length base change.
- **step-ordinary-norm** [claim](#stmt-ds-ordinary-norm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ds-ordinary-norm): Koszul splitting for finiteness, agreement on base classes by spectral sequences, then descent to invariant classes. -> Equality of ordinary and normalized norm sums.
- **step-supported-subquotient** [claim](#stmt-as-supported-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-as-supported-quotient): Factor maps from supported to small objects through supported small objects, then roof argument. -> Fully faithful supported subquotient.
- **step-evaluations** [claim](#stmt-as-evaluations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-as-evaluations): Koszul splitting bounds homology on supported lifts, small objects are killed, extension to summands through images of idempotents. -> Homological functors, Euler homomorphisms and the Koszul polynomial identity.
- **step-asymptotic-finite-tor** [claim](#stmt-as-finite-tor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-as-finite-tor): Reuse the Koszul and composition-series argument, which needs no completeness. -> Finite Tor statement over the present base.
- **step-base-equality** [claim](#stmt-as-base-equality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-as-base-equality): Descend two finite spectral sequences through supported roofs and take images of the idempotent on each page. -> Both evaluations equal the rank times the base evaluation.
- **step-connective** [claim](#stmt-as-connective) / [proof](#proof-stmt-as-connective): Additive generator gives a product of matrix algebras; flatness and an explicit dg quotient give a connective endomorphism algebra. -> Perfect module description by a connective algebra.
- **step-asymptotic-norm** [claim](#stmt-as-norm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-as-norm): Apply supported descent to the connective algebra and use base equality on the image from the base. -> Asymptotic norm comparison.
- **step-reduction** [claim](#stmt-pos-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pos-reduction): Serre's equicharacteristic case, completion and residue extension, vanishing in smaller dimensions, prime filtrations. -> Reduction to complete domains of complementary positive dimensions.
- **step-first-average** [claim](#stmt-pos-first-average) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pos-first-average): Apply the ordinary norm comparison to a supported free complex and compare ranks through torsion cokernels. -> First Galois average identity.
- **step-tor-bound** [claim](#stmt-pos-tor-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pos-tor-bound): Change-of-rings spectral sequence with vanishing Koszul rows, then a splitting argument for the degree bound. -> Identification of Tor lengths and vanishing above the codimension degree.
- **step-approximation** [claim](#stmt-pos-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pos-approximation): Finitely presented approximations of the quotient module, error control through long exact sequences, descent to a tower stage. -> Finite-length modules with common annihilator and positive generator bound.
- **step-supported-truncation** [claim](#stmt-pos-supported-truncation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pos-supported-truncation): Brutal truncation with error factoring through a negligible module, support from the common annihilator, Koszul comparison. -> Supported sequence whose ordinary evaluation equals the first-average term.
- **step-positive-evaluation** [claim](#stmt-pos-positive-evaluation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pos-positive-evaluation): Koszul rows vanish so only degree zero survives; a surjection onto residue quotients and the generator bound give strict positivity. -> Positive lower bound for the normalized evaluation.
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pos-positive-evaluation-2): Combine the two averages with positivity of the normalized evaluations and the reduction. -> Strict positivity of Serre's intersection multiplicity.
- **step-tangent-cone-corollary** [claim](#stmt-cor-tangent-cone) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-tangent-cone): Blowup at the closed point, finite-support excess formula, positivity of each local multiplicity from the main theorem. -> Lower bound and equality criterion.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-strict-positivity**: For nonzero finitely generated modules over a regular local ring whose tensor product has finite length and whose dimensions add up to the ring dimension, Serre's intersection multiplicity is strictly positive.
- **inv-tangent-cone-lower-bound**: For prime quotients of a regular local ring with complementary dimensions, the intersection multiplicity is at least the product of the two Hilbert-Samuel multiplicities, with equality exactly when the tensor product of the associated graded rings has dimension zero.
- **inv-asymptotic-norm-comparison**: Summed over the group, the ordinary and normalized Euler evaluations agree on every supported object of the rank quotient category, without invariance of the length under the group.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-subcomplementary-dimensions**: Expect a positive intersection multiplicity when the dimensions of the two modules add up to less than the ring dimension. -> In that range the multiplicity vanishes, so positivity fails.
- **anti-large-tangent-cone**: Apply the product-of-multiplicities lower bound when the tensor product of associated graded rings has dimension larger than one. -> The finite-support blowup argument that yields the bound is no longer available.
- **anti-length-invariance**: Assume the normalized length is invariant under the Galois group and compare individual conjugate evaluations. -> Only sums over conjugates are compared; individual evaluations need not agree.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are existence and positivity statements proved through normalized lengths, ultrafilter limits of rank sequences and descent in rational K-theory; the source gives no finite procedure that computes an object, so no runnable reference implementation with an independent oracle exists.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper is an existential proof of strict positivity of a Tor Euler characteristic. It specifies no program, interface or executable procedure that software could implement. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Regular local rings, Tor groups and K-theory spectra have no state, dynamics or sampling content that a simulation could reproduce. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No tool-level procedure is given. The only practical use is as a theoretical sanity guard for a computer algebra system, and that is a use of the conclusion, not a construction from the paper. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof passes through ultrafilter limits, Verdier quotients, idempotent completions and K-theory descent. These are non-constructive steps, so no finite procedure with complexity is extracted. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No representation, index or container is defined. The categories and algebras built in the paper are abstract mathematical objects with no storage or lookup semantics. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no dataflow, arithmetic kernel or numerical pipeline in the paper, so nothing maps to hardware. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Perfectoid and Witt-vector machinery appears only as local coefficient tools for a commutative-algebra proof. No hardness assumption, protocol or security claim is made. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no data, loss function, learning rule or statistical model. Limits are taken along sequences of modules, not training sets. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is a strict inequality for an Euler characteristic, not an optimization problem. The bounds used inside the proof are lower bounds with no objective or feasible set to optimize. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Group actions occur only as finite Galois symmetries in a descent argument. There are no processes, messages, faults or consensus. |
| other (pure mathematics: commutative algebra and algebraic K-theory) | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The genuine home of the paper is theoretical mathematics, where it settles Serre's positivity conjecture. That is a research contribution, not an engineering construction, so no engineering contract is claimed here. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-regular-local-ring** (hypothesis): The positivity theorem is stated only for a commutative Noetherian regular local ring, where regularity means the maximal ideal is generated by d elements. Blocked use: ['Applying strict positivity of the Tor Euler characteristic over singular or non-regular local rings']
- **limit-finite-length-tensor** (hypothesis): The tensor product of the two modules must have finite length; otherwise the Euler characteristic is not defined by the source. Blocked use: ['Using the theorem for modules whose supports meet in positive dimension']
- **limit-complementary-dimension** (edge_case): Strict positivity is claimed only when the dimensions add up exactly to the ring dimension; for a smaller sum the earlier vanishing theorem applies and the multiplicity is zero. Blocked use: ['Expecting positive multiplicity when the dimension sum is below d']
- **limit-tangent-cone-dimension** (hypothesis): The lower bound by the product of Hilbert–Samuel multiplicities, with its equality criterion, is only asserted when the tensor product of associated graded rings has dimension at most one. Blocked use: ['Applying the multiplicity-product bound when the tangent cone intersection has larger dimension']
- **limit-nonconstructive-limits** (nonconstructive): The asymptotic evaluations use limits along a nonprincipal ultrafilter and the supremum property of normalized length to choose approximations, so no finite procedure or complexity bound is produced. Blocked use: ['Extracting an algorithm or runtime guarantee for computing the intersection multiplicity from the proof']
- **limit-no-group-invariance** (formal_scope): Only sums over Galois conjugates of the ordinary and normalized evaluations are compared; no invariance of the normalized length under the group is available for individual classes. Blocked use: ['Comparing individual conjugate evaluations as if the length were group invariant']
- **limit-connective-coefficients** (hypothesis): The descent theorem requires the coefficient algebra to be connective, and the proof says this connectivity is essential. Blocked use: ['Applying supported rational descent to coefficient algebras with nonvanishing negative homotopy']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove Serre's positivity conjecture, including ramified mixed characteristic. If two nonzero finitely generated modules over a regular local ring have tensor product of finite length and complementary dimensions, then their intersection multiplicity is strictly positive.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $(R,\mathfrak{m})$ be a commutative Noetherian regular local ring of dimension $d$. For finite $R$-modules $M,N$ whose tensor product has finite length, Serre’s intersection multiplicity is the integer $$\chi^R(M,N)=\sum_{i=0}^{d}(-1)^i
       \operatorname{length}_R\operatorname{Tor}_i^R(M,N).$$ The support condition makes every term finite, and regularity makes the higher Tor groups vanish. This Euler characteristic measures the local intersection of the supports while accounting for higher Tor. Its positivity is not a formal consequence of the nonzero degree-zero term: the higher terms enter with alternating signs.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $(R,\mathfrak{m})$ be a commutative Noetherian regular local ring of dimension $d$, and let $M,N$ be nonzero finitely generated $R$-modules. If $\operatorname{length}_R(M\otimes_R N)<\infty$ and $\dim_R M+\dim_R N=d$, then $$\chi^R(M,N)>0.$$*

</div>

Here regularity means that $\mathfrak{m}$ can be generated by $d$ elements, and module dimension means the Krull dimension of its support. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> proves Serre’s positivity conjecture without a restriction on characteristic or ramification. In particular, it does not require either module to lift to an unramified regular local ring.[^1]

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Background and significance

Serre introduced intersection multiplicities through the Euler characteristic of Tor. His reduction to the diagonal proves positivity in equicharacteristic and in the unramified setting; the same argument also treats formal power series rings over an arbitrary complete discrete valuation ring <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Serre](#ref-Serre), Chapter V]. Thus ramification alone does not separate the known cases from the general problem. Roberts and, independently, Gillet–Soulé proved vanishing when the tensor product has finite length and the sum of dimensions is smaller than the ring dimension <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Roberts](#ref-Roberts), [RobertsRennes](#ref-RobertsRennes), [GilletSoule](#ref-GilletSoule)]. Their arguments use local Chern characters and Adams operations, respectively. Gabber proved nonnegativity in the remaining mixed-characteristic case using alterations and an intersection calculation on a normal bundle <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Gabber](#ref-Gabber), Section 6.1, Theorem 6.1.1]. Vanishing removes lower-dimensional contributions in the reductions below. For complementary-dimensional pairs, strict positivity must additionally rule out zero.

Further progress in ramified mixed characteristic includes Skalit’s positivity theorem for regular local rings essentially smooth over a two-dimensional regular local base <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Skalit](#ref-Skalit)]. KC and Soto Levins prove positivity for modules satisfying a Serre-liftability condition <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[KCSotoLevins](#ref-KCSotoLevins), Theorem A]. In their positivity statement, $R=S/(f)$ for an unramified regular local ring $S$ and $f\in\mathfrak{m}_S\setminus\mathfrak{m}_S^2$, and $M$ has a Serre lift $L$: a finite $S$-module with $M=L\otimes_S R$ and $\dim S-\dim L=\dim R-\dim M$; it need not satisfy the usual Tor-vanishing condition for a lift. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> imposes neither this liftability condition nor the smoothness hypothesis.

The proof combines normalized-length methods with algebraic $K$-theory. Faltings introduced the normalized-length formalism in almost mathematics <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Faltings](#ref-Faltings), Appendix 2]; Gabber–Ramero developed its almost-ring-theoretic foundations further <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[GabberRamero](#ref-GabberRamero)]. The perfectoid length results of Cai–Lee–Ma–Schwede–Tucker <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[CLMST](#ref-CLMST)], together with their characteristic-$p$ counterparts <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Ma](#ref-Ma)], give algebras on which parameter Koszul complexes are exact in positive degrees after passing to normalized length. The mixed-characteristic construction uses perfectoidization and almost purity, as developed by Bhatt–Scholze <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BhattScholze](#ref-BhattScholze)]. Land–Tamme’s descent theorem for truncating invariants <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[LT](#ref-LT)] supplies the categorical descent input. Bhatt–Hochster–Ma show that the existence of lim Cohen–Macaulay sequences for both quotient domains implies positivity <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[BHM](#ref-BHM), Theorem 6.1]. Ishiro–Shimomoto obtain such sequences of algebras from perfectoid towers over complete mixed-characteristic Noetherian local domains with $F$-finite residue field <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[IshiroShimomoto](#ref-IshiroShimomoto), Theorem 3.17]. These are conditional routes through finite modules. Here no such tower on either quotient domain is assumed: the proof uses bounded complexes modulo free sequences of negligible normalized rank.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof strategy

After standard reductions, it is enough to consider two complete local domains $D=R/P$ and $E=R/Q$ of positive dimensions $h,l$, where $h+l=d$ and $P+Q$ is $\mathfrak{m}$-primary. Each domain has a finite extension, denoted $D'$ or $E'$, with a finite Galois action over a regular normalization base $A_D$ or $A_E$, that is, a regular local subring over which the extension is finite. Associated algebras $C_D,C_E$ carry additive nonnegative lengths $\lambda_D,\lambda_E$. Their parameter quotients have positive length, whereas positive parameter Koszul homology has length zero.

There are two distinct comparisons in the argument. First, rational $K$-theory with support at the closed point shows that ordinary and normalized Euler lengths agree after summing over all Galois conjugates. Write $G_D,G_E$ for the two groups, and $a_D=\operatorname{rank}_D D'$, $a_E=\operatorname{rank}_E E'$. For $\sigma\in G_D$, let $c_\sigma$ be the normalized Tor Euler characteristic of $C_D$ and $E'$ over $R$, where the $R$-action on $C_D$ is twisted by $\sigma$. The first comparison identifies the sum of the $c_\sigma$ with $|G_D|a_Da_E\chi^R(D,E)$. It therefore suffices to show that each $c_\sigma$ is positive. Fix one such twist.

To analyze that value, choose a regular sequence $x$ in $Q$ that is a parameter system on $D$. The module $U=C_D/xC_D$ has positive finite normalized length over $B=R/(x)$. It can be approximated by finite-length $B$-modules $M_n$ killed by one fixed power of the maximal ideal of $B$, with ordinary lengths divided by integers $s_n$ converging to the relevant normalized lengths. Their minimal resolutions have ranks $o(s_n)$ above degree $l$. Truncating at degree $l$ therefore produces supported complexes over $E'$ in the category that kills free sequences whose ranks divided by $s_n$ tend to zero.

The second comparison takes place in this category. Its supported objects have nonnegative homology lengths, even when support appears only after passing to the quotient. The two Euler evaluations again agree on sums of conjugates. Evaluation using $C_E$ leaves only degree zero, whose length is bounded below by a positive constant times the normalized number of generators of $M_n$. The common annihilator and the positive limiting length of $M_n$ make this lower bound strictly positive. The second Galois average proves positivity for each value in the first average, completing the argument.

Figure <a href="#fig:two-comparisons" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:two-comparisons">1</a> records the two interfaces. Here $b_{\sigma,\gamma}$ denotes the $C_E$-evaluation of the truncated sequence after the additional twist $\gamma\in G_E$. Choose $a$ with $\mathfrak{m}_B^aM_n=0$ for all $n$, and write $\beta_0(M_n)$ for the minimal number of generators of $M_n$. Then $\operatorname{length}_B M_n\leq\beta_0(M_n)\operatorname{length}_B(B/\mathfrak{m}_B^a)$. Thus the positive normalized length of the $M_n$ forces a positive normalized number of generators; it is this uniform estimate that makes every $b_{\sigma,\gamma}$ strictly positive.

<figure id="fig:two-comparisons">

</tr>

The first is Lemma <a href="#pos:first-average" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pos:first-average">23</a>; the second is Equation <a href="#pos:second-average" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pos:second-average">[pos:second-average]</a>. The uniform annihilator and generator bound give the strict inequality in Lemma <a href="#pos:positive-evaluation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pos:positive-evaluation">27</a>.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-two-comparisons"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:two-comparisons`)

Visual witness description of PDF page 3 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):

```text
A vertical flow diagram showing two comparisons in framed boxes connected by an arrow:
- Top rectangular box with title embedded in the top border: "Ordinary supported comparison over $A_D \subset D'$", containing the centered equation:
$$|G_D| a_D a_E \chi^R(D, E) = \sum_{\sigma \in G_D} c_\sigma$$
- Between the boxes, an upward vertical arrow ($\uparrow$) on the left with the text label to its right: "prove positivity separately for each $\sigma$".
- Bottom rectangular box with title embedded in the top border: "Asymptotic supported comparison over $A_E \subset E'$", containing the centered equation:

```


The reusable technical points are the finite-surjection descent argument with arbitrary connective algebra coefficients and the construction of supported Euler evaluations after quotienting by negligible ranks. In particular, the comparison is proved on idempotent summands in the quotient, not only on complexes that already have uniform support before taking the quotient. No invariance of a normalized-length function under either Galois group is assumed.

Section <a href="#sec:lengths" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:lengths">2</a> establishes the precise normalized-length inputs. Section <a href="#sec:descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:descent">3</a> proves supported rational descent and the first Euler comparison. Section <a href="#sec:asymptotic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:asymptotic">4</a> constructs the rank quotient and its supported evaluations. Section <a href="#sec:positivity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:positivity">5</a> performs the approximation and the two comparisons to prove Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Consequences

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> also has consequences for symbolic powers. For a ramified mixed-characteristic regular local ring $(R,\mathfrak{m})$ and primes $P,Q$ with $\sqrt{P+Q}=\mathfrak{m}$ and $\operatorname{ht}P+\operatorname{ht}Q=\dim R$, the implication of Kurano–Roberts <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[KuranoRoberts](#ref-KuranoRoberts), Theorem 3.2] now gives $$P\cap Q^{(r)}\subseteq\mathfrak{m}^{r+1}\qquad(r\geq1),
 \qquad Q^{(r)}:=Q^rR_Q\cap R.$$ Here ramified means that the residue characteristic $p$ lies in $\mathfrak{m}^2$. This is a direct application of their positivity-to-symbolic-power theorem, rather than an additional ingredient in our proof.

The next consequence applies Skalit’s finite-support blowup argument <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Skalit](#ref-Skalit), Corollary D] with Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> as its positivity input. Dutta studied the lower bound in this finite-support setting <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[DuttaFiniteSupport](#ref-DuttaFiniteSupport)]. We include the argument to exhibit how strict positivity at the points of the blowup controls equality.

<a id="stmt-cor-tangent-cone"></a>
<div id="cor:tangent-cone" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Finite strict-transform intersection). *Let $(A,\mathfrak{m})$ be a commutative Noetherian regular local ring with residue field $k$, and let $P,Q$ be prime ideals such that $A/(P+Q)$ has finite length and $\dim(A/P)+\dim(A/Q)=\dim A$. Write $e_\mathfrak{m}$ for Hilbert–Samuel multiplicity and put $$T=\operatorname{gr}(A/P)\otimes_{\operatorname{gr}A}\operatorname{gr}(A/Q),$$ where all associated graded rings use the maximal-ideal filtrations. If $\dim T\leq1$, then $$\chi^A(A/P,A/Q)\geq e_\mathfrak{m}(A/P)e_\mathfrak{m}(A/Q),$$ with equality if and only if $\dim T=0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-tangent-cone"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-tangent-cone</summary>

*Proof.* If $\dim A=0$, then $A=k$, $P=Q=0$, and both sides are $1$ with $T=k$. Otherwise, let $\widetilde X$ be the blowup of $X=\operatorname{Spec}A$ at its closed point, and let $\widetilde Y,\widetilde Z$ be the strict transforms of $Y=\operatorname{Spec}(A/P)$ and $Z=\operatorname{Spec}(A/Q)$. By <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[SkalitUnramified](#ref-SkalitUnramified), Lemma 3.5], the underlying set $S$ of $\widetilde Y\cap\widetilde Z$ identifies with that of $\operatorname{Proj}T$. Thus $S$ is a finite set of closed points of the exceptional divisor, empty exactly when $\dim T=0$. Fulton’s blowup formula and Skalit’s finite-support calculation <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[SkalitUnramified](#ref-SkalitUnramified), Theorem 3.4 and proof of Proposition 3.10] give $$\chi^A(A/P,A/Q)-e_\mathfrak{m}(A/P)e_\mathfrak{m}(A/Q)
   =\sum_{x\in S}[\kappa(x):k]\,\chi^{B_x}(M_x,N_x),$$ where $B_x=\mathcal O_{\widetilde X,x}$, $M_x=\mathcal O_{\widetilde Y,x}$, and $N_x=\mathcal O_{\widetilde Z,x}$. Each residue degree is finite and positive since the exceptional divisor is projective over $k$. The blowup $\widetilde X$ is regular <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Jannsen](#ref-Jannsen), Proposition A.4(1)]. The modules $M_x,N_x$ are nonzero and finite over $B_x$, with finite-length tensor product because their intersection is supported on $S$. The local domains $A/P$ and $A/Q$, as well as $A$, are formally equidimensional and universally catenary. Thus <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[SkalitUnramified](#ref-SkalitUnramified), Lemma 3.8 and proof of Proposition 3.10] gives $\dim M_x=\dim(A/P)$, $\dim N_x=\dim(A/Q)$, and $\dim B_x=\dim A$. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> therefore makes every local intersection multiplicity in the sum strictly positive. The asserted inequality follows, and equality holds exactly when $S$ is empty. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Conventions

We fix universes large enough for the module and sequence categories under consideration. Complexes are indexed homologically. An associative derived algebra is *connective* when its homotopy groups in negative degrees vanish. Perfect modules over associative algebras are right modules. All scalar localizations of coefficient algebras are along the central image of the commutative base. We use nonconnective algebraic $K$-theory of perfect complexes; a subscript $\mathbb{Q}$ denotes rationalization. For a sequence $a=(a_1,\ldots,a_r)$, write $K(a;V)$ for its homological Koszul complex with coefficients in $V$, and put $H_i(a;V)=H_i(K(a;V))$. The notation $a^v$ denotes $(a_1^v,\ldots,a_r^v)$, not the $v$th power of the ideal it generates. Normalized lengths are allowed to vanish on nonzero modules; all uses of their additivity involve finite values or nonnegative extended values for which the indicated operations are defined.

<a id="sec:lengths"></a>
### Residue fields and normalized length

The comparison arguments require algebras on which positive parameter Koszul homology has length zero and parameter quotients have prescribed positive length. The finite approximations in Section <a href="#sec:positivity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:positivity">5</a> also require that this length be defined on underlying modules over a flat tower, without an action of the full algebra. We construct these objects after arranging a perfect residue field; neither unramifiedness nor Cohen–Macaulayness of the domain is required. Both characteristics are needed: a prime quotient of the mixed-characteristic ambient ring may itself have characteristic $p$, even though ambient equicharacteristic positivity is already known.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Extending the residue field

<a id="stmt-len-residue-extension"></a>
<div id="len:residue-extension" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Residue-field extension). *Let $(B,\mathfrak b,k)$ be a complete Noetherian local ring, and let $\overline{k}$ be an algebraic closure of $k$. There is a complete Noetherian local ring $(B',\mathfrak b',\overline{k})$ and a flat local map $B\to B'$ such that $\mathfrak bB'=\mathfrak b'$. For every finite $B$-module $V$ and every $a\geq1$, $$\operatorname{length}_{B'}\bigl((V\otimes_B B')/(\mathfrak b')^a
                          (V\otimes_B B')\bigr)
       =\operatorname{length}_B(V/\mathfrak b^aV).$$ Consequently module dimensions are preserved, as are finite lengths. If $B$ is regular, then $B'$ is regular. For finite $B$-modules $M,N$ whose tensor product has finite length, every Tor length, and hence their intersection multiplicity when defined, is preserved.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-len-residue-extension"><summary>Proof of stmt-len-residue-extension</summary>

*Proof.* In equal characteristic, a coefficient field gives a presentation $B=k[[X_1,\ldots,X_t]]/I$. Put $B'=\overline{k}[[X_1,\ldots,X_t]]/I\overline{k}[[X_1,\ldots,X_t]]$. The map of power-series rings is flat, by the local flatness criterion for the variables and the flat coefficient-field extension. Its base change to the quotient has the asserted residue field and maximal ideal.

In the remaining case the residue characteristic is a prime $p$. Cohen structure supplies a presentation $B=V[[X_1,\ldots,X_t]]/I$, where $V$ is a complete discrete valuation ring with uniformizer $p$ and residue field $k$. We construct a discrete valuation extension with unchanged uniformizer and residue field $\overline{k}$, without assuming that $k$ is perfect. Given a simple algebraic residue-field extension, lift its monic irreducible polynomial to $f\in V[X]$. The algebra $V[X]/(f)$ is finite free over $V$, its reduction modulo $p$ is the desired field, and all its maximal ideals lie over $(p)$. It is therefore local with maximal ideal generated by $p$. Since $p$ is a nonzerodivisor, it is a one-dimensional regular local ring, hence a discrete valuation ring. This argument also applies to inseparable residue extensions.

Perform this construction along a tower of simple algebraic extensions exhausting $\overline{k}$, taking unions at limit stages. In every such union, a nonzero element is a unit times a nonnegative integral power of $p$. Every nonzero ideal is generated by an element of least valuation, so the union is still a discrete valuation ring. Its completion $V'$ has uniformizer $p$ and residue field $\overline{k}$. The map $V\to V'$ is flat because $V'$ is torsion-free over $V$. Now take $$B'=V'[[X_1,\ldots,X_t]]/IV'[[X_1,\ldots,X_t]].$$ The same local flatness criterion proves the required flatness. This is the usual residue-extension construction; compare <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Ma](#ref-Ma), Lemma 2.2].

For either construction, tensor a composition series of a finite-length $B$-module with $B'$. Flatness preserves exactness, and every simple factor becomes $B'/\mathfrak b'$. This proves the length assertions and, applied to $V/\mathfrak b^aV$, the displayed identity. Equality of Hilbert–Samuel functions gives equality of module dimensions. If $B$ is regular of dimension $d$, its $d$ maximal-ideal generators generate $\mathfrak b'$, and $\dim B'=d$, so $B'$ is regular. Finally flat base change identifies the Tor modules after extension; their finite lengths are preserved by the preceding argument. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Regular bases and the tower length

For a perfect field $k$ of characteristic $p$, let $W(k)$ denote its ring of Witt vectors.

<a id="stmt-len-normalization"></a>
<div id="len:normalization" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Regular normalization bases). *Let $(D,\mathfrak d,k)$ be a complete Noetherian local domain of dimension $h>0$, where $k$ is perfect of characteristic $p>0$. There is a finite, generically separable inclusion $A\subseteq D$, inducing the identity on residue fields, with $$A=k[[t_1,\ldots,t_h]]\quad\text{if }\operatorname{char}D=p,
 \qquad
 A=W(k)[[t_2,\ldots,t_h]]\quad\text{if }\operatorname{char}D=0.$$ In the second case set $t_1=p$. In both cases $t_1,\ldots,t_h$ is a parameter system of $D$ and a regular parameter system of $A$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-len-normalization"><summary>Proof of stmt-len-normalization</summary>

*Proof.* The positive-characteristic assertion is the Cohen–Gabber normalization theorem <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[CohenGabber](#ref-CohenGabber)]. In mixed characteristic, choose a coefficient ring $W(k)\subseteq D$ and extend $p$ to a parameter system $p,t_2,\ldots,t_h$. Completeness and the parameter condition make the resulting power-series map module finite. Its kernel is zero: the source and target have dimension $h$, whereas a nonzero ideal of the regular local domain $W(k)[[t_2,\ldots,t_h]]$ lowers dimension. The fraction-field extension is separable in characteristic zero. ◻

</details>

<a id="len:tower-definition"></a>
Fix one of the bases in Lemma <a href="#len:normalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="len:normalization">4</a> and compatible $p$-power roots of its parameters. Write $$\label{len:tower-definition}
 A_n=A[t_1^{1/p^n},\ldots,t_h^{1/p^n}],\qquad
 \Lambda=\varinjlim_n A_n.$$ The tower is uncompleted. Only in mixed characteristic do we also use its $p$-adic completion, denoted $A_\infty$.

<a id="stmt-len-tower"></a>
<div id="len:tower" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (The tower and its length). *Each $A_n$ is a complete regular local ring with residue field $k$. For $b\geq a$, the map $A_a\to A_b$ is finite free of rank $p^{(b-a)h}$; in particular $A\to A_n$ has rank $p^{nh}$. On the category of $\Lambda$-modules killed by some power of $\mathfrak m_A$ there is a nonnegative additive function $\lambda_\infty$ with the following properties.*

<a id="len:finite-stage"></a>
1.  *A finitely presented module descends to a finite-length $A_n$-module $V_n$, and $$\label{len:finite-stage}
           \lambda_\infty(V_n\otimes_{A_n}\Lambda)
                          =p^{-nh}\operatorname{length}_{A_n}(V_n).$$*

2.  *For a finitely generated module $V$, its length is the infimum of the lengths of finitely presented modules in this category admitting a surjection onto $V$.*

<a id="len:supremum"></a>
3.  *For an arbitrary module $V$ in this category, $$\label{len:supremum}
           \lambda_\infty(V)=
           \sup\{\lambda_\infty(V_0):V_0\subseteq V
                                      \text{ is finitely generated}\}.$$*

*The value on an arbitrary module may be infinite. In mixed characteristic, restriction from $A_\infty$ to $\Lambda$ gives the same length on modules killed by a fixed power of $\mathfrak m_A$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-len-tower"><summary>Proof of stmt-len-tower</summary>

*Proof.* In characteristic $p$, the stages are power-series rings in the chosen roots. In mixed characteristic they are power-series rings over the complete discrete valuation ring $W(k)[p^{1/p^n}]$. The monomials in the successive roots, with each exponent less than the relevant root degree, form bases for all transition extensions. This proves the assertions about regularity, ranks, and residue fields.

Finite presentations over the union descend to a stage. The additional relations expressing annihilation by a fixed power of $\mathfrak m_A$ also descend after increasing that stage, because there are finitely many generators and relations to consider. The resulting finite-stage module has finite length. Formula <a href="#len:finite-stage" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="len:finite-stage">[len:finite-stage]</a> is unchanged on enlarging the stage, by flatness and the computed free ranks. The infimum and supremum extensions, including their additivity and compatibility with this formula, are Faltings’ normalized-length formalism <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Faltings](#ref-Faltings), Appendix 2], in the formulation of <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[CLMST](#ref-CLMST), Definition 2.2.1 and Propositions 2.2.3, 2.2.6(a)]. The same residue field implies that $A_n$-length and $A$-length agree on finite-length $A_n$-modules.

In mixed characteristic $\Lambda$ is $p$-torsion-free, and the natural map induces $\Lambda/p^a\Lambda\simeq A_\infty/p^aA_\infty$ for every $a\geq1$. A module killed by $\mathfrak m_A^a$ is killed by $p^a$. Its submodules and finite generating sets are therefore identical over the two bases. In computing the infimum over finitely presented covers of such a module, one may replace a cover $V$ by $V/\mathfrak m_A^aV$; this is still finitely presented. These covers also correspond over the identical quotient rings, proving the last assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-len-zero-length-limits"></a>
<div id="len:zero-length-limits" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6** (Zero-length limits). *Suppose all modules under consideration are killed by one fixed power of $\mathfrak m_A$. Sums, direct sums, and directed colimits of zero-length modules have length zero. For an increasing union, $$\lambda_\infty\Bigl(\bigcup_a V_a\Bigr)
                         =\sup_a\lambda_\infty(V_a).$$ If $(V_a)_{a\geq1}$ is a direct system with $\lambda_\infty(V_a)\to0$, then $\lambda_\infty(\varinjlim_a V_a)=0$, without any injectivity assumption on the transition maps.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-len-zero-length-limits"><summary>Proof of stmt-len-zero-length-limits</summary>

*Proof.* A finite set of elements of a direct sum is contained in a finite subsum. Additivity and <a href="#len:supremum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="len:supremum">[len:supremum]</a> give the assertion for direct sums; sums and directed colimits are quotients of direct sums. For an increasing union, every finite generating set lies in one stage. Finally a finitely generated submodule of $\varinjlim_a V_a$ is contained in the image of every sufficiently late $V_a$, by lifting its generators to a common stage. Monotonicity bounds its length by $\lambda_\infty(V_a)$ at all such stages. Apply <a href="#len:supremum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="len:supremum">[len:supremum]</a>. The common annihilator ensures throughout that these modules belong to the length category. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Length algebras and parameter homology

The tower length will now measure modules over an algebra of the given domain. Its parameter identities have two uses: the base parameters control the Euler comparisons, while arbitrary parameter systems give the positive quotients used in Section <a href="#sec:positivity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:positivity">5</a>. For an ideal $I$ primary to the maximal ideal of a local ring $D$, write $e(I,D)$ for its Hilbert–Samuel multiplicity.

Related normalized-length constructions, in which parameter-quotient lengths recover multiplicities and positive parameter Koszul homology has normalized length zero, appear in <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[OpenAILech2026](#ref-OpenAILech2026), Theorem 3.1]. Here we retain the chosen base $A\subseteq D$ and the finite-stage, infimum, and supremum properties on underlying tower modules.

<a id="stmt-len-input"></a>
<div id="len:input" class="theorem">

<a id="len:koszul-vanishing"></a>
<a id="len:parameter-length"></a>
<a id="len:base-parameter-length"></a>
**Theorem 7** (Normalized-length input). *Let $(D,\mathfrak d,k)$ and a chosen finite generically separable base $A\subseteq D$ be as in Lemma <a href="#len:normalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="len:normalization">4</a>, and let $\Lambda$ be the tower <a href="#len:tower-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="len:tower-definition">[len:tower-definition]</a>. There is a $D$-algebra $C_D$, carrying a compatible $\Lambda$-action, such that restriction of scalars defines an additive nonnegative length $\lambda_D=\lambda_\infty$ on its modules annihilated by a power of $\mathfrak d$. For every parameter system $z_1,\ldots,z_h$ of $D$ and every integer $v\geq1$, $$\begin{aligned}
 \lambda_D H_i(z_1^v,\ldots,z_h^v;C_D)&=0\qquad(i>0),
                                      \label{len:koszul-vanishing}\\
 \lambda_D\bigl(C_D/(z_1^v,\ldots,z_h^v)C_D\bigr)
                    &=v^h e((z_1,\ldots,z_h),D).
                                      \label{len:parameter-length}
\end{aligned}$$ The right side is positive and finite. The algebra and length are independent of the parameter system $z$. For the regular parameters of $A$, in particular, $$\label{len:base-parameter-length}
 \lambda_D\bigl(C_D/(t_1^v,\ldots,t_h^v)C_D\bigr)
                         =v^h\operatorname{rank}_A D.$$ The finite-stage, infimum, and supremum properties of Lemma <a href="#len:tower" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="len:tower">5</a> remain available on underlying tower modules; they do not require that the relevant submodules or covers be $C_D$-modules.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-len-input"><summary>Proof of stmt-len-input</summary>

*Proof.* We give the positive-characteristic construction and then specify the mixed-characteristic literature inputs.

Assume first that $D$ has characteristic $p$, and put $C_D=D_{\mathrm{perf}}=\bigcup_n D^{1/p^n}$ inside a fixed algebraic closure of its fraction field. Here $\Lambda=A_{\mathrm{perf}}$. Write $K=\operatorname{Frac}(A)$, $L=\operatorname{Frac}(D)$, and $K'=K_{\mathrm{perf}}$. For $q=p^n$ put $$B_q=\Lambda\otimes_{A^{1/q}}D^{1/q}.$$ Flatness of $\Lambda$ over $A^{1/q}$ and torsion-freeness of $D^{1/q}$ embed this tensor product into $K'\otimes_{K^{1/q}}L^{1/q}$. Generic separability makes the latter a field, by linear disjointness from a purely inseparable extension. Thus $B_q=\Lambda D^{1/q}\subseteq C_D$, and these subrings increase to $C_D$.

<a id="len:conductor"></a>
Let $H$ be any $\mathfrak d$-primary ideal and set $V_q=B_q/HB_q$ and $W_q=\operatorname{im}(V_q\to C_D/HC_D)$. Write $H^{[q]}=(a^q:a\in H)$ for its Frobenius power. Finite-stage normalization and the $q$-th-power isomorphism give $$\lambda_\infty(V_q)=q^{-h}\operatorname{length}_D(D/H^{[q]}).$$ Although $B_q\subseteq C_D$, the map on these quotients need not be injective.
